        254. d598eff7da  PR #44776 (open, head 5e26a3473e, ported)  optional parallel page READS in the file backend -- SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_BACKEND_READ_WORKERS, DEFAULT OFF (EnvInt(1) = serial), order-preserving, raises-become-misses, and unioned with our entry-24 page-degrade catch

      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/44776. WHAT
      # (hicache_storage.py ~+77/-14 + environ.py +2 + docs row +5): with
      # read_workers > 1, _map_reads runs the FILE READS of one
      # batch_get/batch_get_v2 call on a lazily built thread pool
      # ("hicache-file-read") and returns only after EVERY read finished,
      # in INPUT ORDER -- so callers, prefetch acks and the cross-rank
      # reduce see results identical to the serial loop. A read that
      # raises becomes a miss (None -> False), never a failed batch.
      # reads_workers <= 1 or len(items) <= 1 short-circuits to the old
      # list-comprehension path -- DEFAULT OFF IS BYTE-IDENTICAL.
      # ARCHITECTURE PIN, not incidental: only the file read (into a
      # private staging page) runs on the pool; every host-pool write
      # (set_from_flat_data_page) STAYS ON THE CALLING THREAD, in page
      # order, because host pools may hold thread-local state there (the
      # unified host pool's layout lease -- calling it from a pool thread
      # can deadlock). Staging is chunked at 4 x read_workers so pool
      # reads never hold one page-buffer per queued item.
      # UNION WITH OUR ENTRY-24 PAGE-DEGRADE WORK -- THE DEVIATION FROM
      # THE PR HEAD, and the reason this entry is marked ported: the PR
      # left the calling-thread pool-write in _read_pages_parallel RAW
      # (an incompatible-layout page would raise out of the daemon
      # storage threads -- the exact failure class entry 24 made
      # per-page-miss). Our port wraps that half in the SAME
      # (OSError, ValueError, RuntimeError) catch + _report_bad_page the
      # serial _read_page uses, so a bad-layout page is a per-page miss
      # on the parallel path too (verified inline, workers=1 and 8); and
      # _batch_io_v2's serial fallback KEEPS our _safe_page_op wrapper
      # the PR had removed. Semantics of the PR preserved: same order,
      # same miss-on-raise, same default.
      # ENTRY-199 WRITE SEMANTICS UNCHANGED, stated plainly because the
      # wanted-file flagged a conflict: batched write-through lives in
      # the tree/scheduler layers and in the file backend's WRITE path --
      # this entry touches READS only; writes stay serial. No overlap in
      # the landed diff.
      # GPU-WINDOW DEBT (both OWED before raising workers on prod):
      # (i) parallel-read end-to-end against the REAL unified host pool
      # -- the CPU test stands in a flat-page pool, not the
      # layout-lease holder; (ii) re-test the upstream DEADLOCK CLAIM
      # the docstring relies on (pool-write-from-pool-thread), so the
      # calling-thread pin is re-verified on our pools, not taken on
      # faith.
      # BATTERY: new test/registered/hicache/
      # test_hicache_file_parallel_reads.py 5P CPU-only
      # (register_cpu_ci, base-a-test-cpu); tranche-wide numbers in the
      # header note.

        285. dd5d2e5898  PR #47051 (open, head f78ee79014, ported)  QSA: keep TWO groups in the pending index-key ring under TARGET_VERIFY -- ** THE REMEDY DECISION: #47051 IS THE CHOSEN REMEDY, #46125 IS THE ORACLE (entry 286) **
      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/47051 (9 files,
      # +143/-40). THE DEFECT: a verify step writes the ring slot of
      # every draft row; when the window does not start on a group
      # boundary, its later rows OVERWRITE members of the group still
      # pending before them -- read back within the step AND, with
      # NOTHING restoring the ring after acceptance, a rejected row's key
      # is read as a pending group member on a LATER step, the corrupted
      # compressed key persisting for the WHOLE REQUEST and skewing
      # indexer block selection. NEXTN 3/1/4 = W4 at ratio 4 is the PR's
      # exact geometry, i.e. ours.
      # ** LAYOUT-EQUIVALENCE PROOF (the reason the PR transfers): ** our
      # ring is ratio * qsa_num_groups rows with a TWO-GROUP FLOOR, not
      # the PR's one-group layout; where the two layouts MEET they are
      # the same function -- at num_groups == 2 our
      # ((p // ratio) % 2) * ratio + p % ratio IS the residue
      # decomposition of p % (2 * ratio), so the PR's remedy lands as the
      # two-group FLOOR plus the shared constant, with every addressing
      # site tied to the pool's qsa_num_groups as the single source of
      # truth. At the prod geometry that is ** 8 SLOTS PER RING **
      # (ratio 4 x 2 groups), collision-free for every parametrized
      # window in the oracle (entry 286).

      # resolves, DODGING the entry-200 import cycle. (2) The PR's RING
      # constexpr hunks in qsa/graph_metadata.py are a NO-OP here --

      # without the attribute cannot under-widen vs the builders. (3) Our

      # [0, ring)); BOTH are inside never-allocated request slot 0's
      # territory -- ours kept per "keep OUR layout". (4) Backend guard:

      # 118-125); the PR's own safety sentence ("a window of up to ratio

        294. 556aea047b  PR #45795 (open, head 6ffe9cd46c, ported)  opt-in async ready-sync (--hicache-async-ready-sync, default False) -- kills a per-step BLOCKING gloo MIN-all-reduce on the scheduler critical path
      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/45795 (7 files,

      # the pending op. The deferred gate REFUSES storage/buffer_only/PP/
      # attn-CP/single-rank setups with a warning, matching the landed
      # _sync_hicache_ready_counts payload (storage-queue sizes stay
      # synchronous).

        308. 3c259eaf0f  STACK-OWNED  LLGUARD: refuse the LL all-reduce when PCIe-IPC all-reduce is ARMED on the same TP group -- promotes entry 256's 5-line dispatch-site COMMENT into an enforced guard
      # NO upstream PR; fork debt from entry 256 (#39605* port). THE
      # COLLISION, AS ENTRY 256 DOCUMENTED IT: the LL path claims bf16
      # <=64 KiB with ** NO CAPTURE GUARD ** while PCIe-IPC AUTOTUNES THE
      # SAME <=64 KiB CAPTURED-BF16 SIZE CLASS on the same TP group, and

      # (entry 245 change 14) -- so entry 256 shipped with an explicit DO-
      # NOT-ARM-BOTH warning and left it to operator discipline. THIS
      # ENTRY MAKES DISCIPLINE STRUCTURAL: illegal states unrepresentable
      # at the construction site rather than a comment a future operator
      # never reads.
      # ** THE PREDICATE IS THE GROUP-LEVEL DECISION, DELIBERATELY NOT THE
      # RANK-LOCAL COMM OBJECT: self.pcie_ipc_eligible -- the stored result
      # of pcie_ipc_ar.eligible_group(), PCIe-IPC's OWN rank-consistent
      # verdict (env AND world_size > 1 AND the "tp" group name AND
      # non-deterministic), not merely the env and NOT the rank-local
      # pcie_ipc_comm. ** WHY: a rank-local setup failure leaves ONE rank
      # holding None while LLAllReduce.__init__ RENDEZVOUSES (barriers on
      # the cpu_group) -- a per-rank refusal would DEADLOCK THE GROUP. So
      # the guard refuses on ALL RANKS OR ON NONE, which is also why an
      # unset env / a non-tp group at ws2 / deterministic inference (cases
      # PCIe-IPC itself declines) all leave LL arming untouched.

  hicliveness-2026-10-29 wave (branch port-hicache-liveness, born at

        318. 29cb4fab8d..b7c656f69f  PR #44342 (open, head 98bfcbd450, ported as 4 commits; NOT in origin/main -- re-verified: grep "(#44342)" = 0 hits)  HiCache storage LIVENESS: recover storage worker collectives, always enter them after a failure, expose liveness, bound the sync timeout, keep the shm row wide enough -- ** THE LIVE PROD PREMISE: ONE SLOW exists() COULD KILL ALL STORAGE WORKERS ON ALL RANKS = SILENT TOTAL L3 LOSS ON OUR TP2 + file-L3 TOPOLOGY **
      # https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/pull/44342 (16 files across
      # the 4 commits: cache_controller.py, hybrid_cache_controller.py,
      # unified_radix_cache.py, shm exchange, parallel_state,
      # metrics_collector + the sync-recovery test).
      # ** THE LIVE PREMISE, QUOTED FROM THE BASE ITSELF (integrator
      # re-verified word-for-word at 78783f0a36, cache_controller.py:1502-
      # 1505): ** "# Outside the guarded query on purpose: every rank must
      # run the same number of hit-count all_reduces or the sync threads
      # deadlock, so a locally failed query still participates (with a
      # zero) instead of skipping the collective." -- and BELOW that
      # comment, the call to _sync_prefetch_hit_query was BARE, i.e. the
      # MIN-reduce itself had NO protection while the design REQUIRES every
      # rank to enter it. So one rank stuck in its backend (a slow
      # exists()) raised inside the collective on that rank only, killed the
      # storage worker on EVERY rank, and left all ranks unable to publish
      # L3 queries or acks: under wait_complete every later L3-hit request
      # waited forever, under timeout every one paid the full deadline.
      # **#43550 guarded the BACKEND CALL, not the COLLECTIVE, and nothing
      # checked worker liveness at all. ** That is why this is a
      # silent-total-L3-loss fix rather than a robustness nicety, and it is
      # exactly the shape prod runs (TP2 + file L3).
      # WHAT LANDS, COMMIT BY COMMIT:
      #  A (29cb4fab8d, from 6fa9f210a8) -- wrap EVERY worker-thread
      #    collective: on failure log ONCE, flag the controller
      #    (storage_sync_failed), PUBLISH THE OPERATION AS FAILED (0 hits, 0
      #    completed tokens) and KEEP THE THREAD ALIVE; after the first
      #    failure the broken groups are skipped so the per-rank
      #    publication sequence stays intact for the queue-count drain. Fold
      #    a RANK-LOCAL HEALTH BIT (no sync failure AND all storage workers
      #    alive) into the MIN-reduce the scheduler-thread drain already
      #    issues; when the AGREED bit drops, apply storage_failure_policy:
      #    "disable" (default) degrades to no-L3 until re-attach (no new
      #    prefetch/backup, in-flight prefetches cut like a deadline,
      #    drained hits read as misses, progress frozen, gauge -> 0),
      #    "raise" fails the scheduler. Adds storage_sync_timeout
      #    extra-config to bound the gloo timeout.
      #  B (45da19f105, from 74a0e6ba9a) -- ** THE ALWAYS-ENTER-POST-FAILURE
      #    INVARIANT, WHICH IS THE DEEPER HALF: ** "skipping every later
      #    collective after the first sync failure was UNSOUND: gloo closes
      #    ONLY THE PAIR OF THE GROUP THAT TIMED OUT, and the hit and
      #    completion sync groups are DISTINCT EVEN ON TP2, so a peer that
      #    had NOT failed blocked for the untouched group's full timeout
      #    (30 MIN BY DEFAULT), during which reset/detach could not join and
      #    flush RAISED OUT OF THE SCHEDULER. A retry on the broken group
      #    raises AT ONCE on every rank, so the collective is now ALWAYS
      #    ENTERED." Also: take_ready_pp_prefetch waits in 1 s slices and
      #    FAILS LOUD when the local ticket thread is dead; the buffer-only
      #    PP>1 health relay is limited to the ticket-less shape (with
      #    tickets, PP0 decides per request); sync knobs validated BEFORE any
      #    attach side effect; the gauge drops ON DETACH; a WARNING once a
      #    minute while degraded; thread attributes initialised in __init__;
      #    reset logs that L3 stays disabled.
      #  C (c50b262e23, from 6c375096cd) -- storage_workers_healthy() watches
      #    the worker threads the controller STARTED (a controller doing
      #    storage I/O OUTSIDE Python workers has none and must read HEALTHY
      #    on their account, not dead); ** storage_sync_timeout DEFAULTS TO
      #    300 s ** instead of torch's 30-minute gloo default (null/absent ->
      #    default, non-finite rejected) -- a hung peer noticed in minutes
      #    while staying far above any healthy batch call; the PP ticket
      #    broadcast group is created WHEN THE STORAGE WORKERS START, after
      #    attach applied the timeout, so it is BOUNDED LIKE THE OTHER
      #    STORAGE COLLECTIVES (it used to be built BEFORE the config was
      #    parsed); a prefetch cut by the rank-agreed degrade reports
      #    ** storage_unavailable ** in storage_prefetch_unfulfilled_tokens_
      #    total INSTEAD OF BORROWING "deadline" (gauge label registered
      #    additively).
      #  D (b7c656f69f, from 98bfcbd450 = the PR head) -- the health bit is
      #    ONE MORE WORD in the HiCache ready-count row, so the no-extra-pool
      #    layout no longer fit the 8-word shm table and ** EVERY
      #    STORAGE-ENABLED TICK FELL BACK TO THE GLOO REDUCE. **
      #    get_shm_exchange takes the row width; the hicache exchange asks
      #    for 9.
      # ** ORDER NOTE -- A CORRECTION TO THE DISPATCH SKETCH, ON THE RECORD:
      # the delegation said "publish-then-flag"; the worker corrected it to

      # AND DOCSTRING: the health bit folds into _sync_hicache_ready_counts
      # AFTER the queue-size reads (flag-published-before-item order: a
      # COUNTED FAILED ITEM IMPLIES THE 0 BIT IN THE SAME ROUND) -- publish-

      # METHOD (the distinction decides the fix). ** The core side was already

      # id, then `arena.node(id).try_parent().map(|p| arena.node(p).id)` ->
      # ** Ok(None) on a root **, which is precisely the interface contract
      # ("The parent node id, or None for the root") and its pure-python twin
      # (unified_cache/unified_tree_core.py:583), and it deliberately uses
      # try_parent() rather than node.rs:219's parent(), which PANICS on a root.
      # The break is placement: B3-7 replanted the BINDING delegate
      # TreeCoreBinding::parent_node_id inside the ** `#[cfg(feature =
      # "inspection")]` ** impl (python_bindings.rs:2314-2324) while the pyclass
      # wrapper method that hands it to Python (3252, emitted per key type by
      # tree_core_binding!) is UNCONDITIONAL -- so any build WITHOUT inspection
      # (i.e. every release/production build; the README states "Production
      # wheels do not enable it") cannot compile. FOUR INDEPENDENT EVIDENCE
      # LINES THAT SETTLE WHICH SIDE IS AUTHORITATIVE (i.e. move it, rather than
      # gate the wrapper -- gating the wrapper would have compiled and then
      # BROKEN PRODUCTION AT RUNTIME):
      #   (i)  `git log -S parent_node_id -- rust/sglang-radix-tree` returns
      #        only a77283fb02/7a464a7014 for the CORE method; the method does
      #        ** NOT EXIST AT THE MERGE #5 BASE (ae6ee527ba) AT ALL ** -- it is
      #        FORK-ONLY, so there was no upstream rename to chase and nothing to
      #        lift from a replant snapshot;
      #   (ii) the fork's own pre-merge copy (2f31a7e3fc, entry-199 era) had the

      #        Oct 28) ** EXPOSES `parent_node_id` AND CONTAINS NO

      #        published the method, which is the runtime contract to preserve;
      #   (iv) the consumer is PRODUCTION: unified_radix_cache.py:4388/4393/4408

      # FIX: delegate moved to the always-on accessor impl beside its siblings

        320. e9f0ae1838  STACK-OWNED FIX (no upstream PR; the warned code is upstream 642e9c1448 / #45791 riding merge #5)  TLFIX: scope-disable TileLang data-race false-positive on the QSA prefill relative kernel -- one per-kernel pass key, zero behavior change, the write-disjointness proof recorded IN THE COMMENT
      # WHAT IT FIXES: the FIRST real request on the rebuilt 9d arm printed,
      # once per TP rank:
      #   Warning: Data race detected: `Logits(row_base + qi, column)` is
      #   written by multiple threads in loop (n,)  ... pass
      #   PassKey.TL_DISABLE_DATA_RACE_CHECK to disable this check
      # KERNEL IDENTITY: `_tilelang_qsa_mqa_prefill_relative_kernel` in
      # python/sglang/srt/layers/attention/qsa/mqa.py -- the only kernel in
      # that file whose loop body names `column`, `ti` and `tile_count`.
      # CITED LINES RE-VERIFIED BY THE INTEGRATOR AND RE-PINNED FOR THIS
      # TREE: the worker cited mqa.py:273 (the def) and :343 (the write), both
      # PRE-PICK; the +12-line insert shifts them, so at THIS tip they are
      # ** def 285 / column expr 350 / guards 353-354 / the write
      # `Logits[row_base + qi, column] = reduced[n, qi]` at 355 **, with the
      # new key at ** 282 ** inside the decorator's pass_configs (266-283).
      # ** ORIGIN -- THIS IS MERGE CARGO, NOT A FORK PICK, WHICH IS WHY IT
      # LOOKED LIKE A 9d-ONLY REGRESSION: ** the kernel entered the stack as
      # upstream ** 642e9c1448 "[Perf] QSA: Optimize prefill indexer in long
      # cached prefixes (#45791)" ** arriving via ** MERGE #5 (ae6ee527ba,
      # main @ 2a6c204570) under stack.txt entry 310 ** -- it was never
      # hand-picked. THAT FULLY EXPLAINS THE 9d-WARNS / 9b-CLEAN SPLIT: the 9b
      # arm still runs the PRE-REBUILD copy, which predates the merge, so it
      # cannot warn about code it does not have. Anyone reading the warning as
      # "fork code went wrong" would chase the wrong tree; the split is a
      # build-vintage artifact, and it will DISAPPEAR from the comparison the
      # moment 9b is rebuilt onto the merged tip.
      # VERDICT: ** CHECKER FALSE-POSITIVE, NOT A REAL RACE ** -- and the
      # proof now lives in the source as an 11-line comment so the next reader
      # does not have to re-derive it. Condensed, the write
      # `Logits[row_base + qi, key_base + n - Starts[row_base + qi]]` is
      # ** injective on (qi, n, ti) per CTA ** by four levels: (1) ** qi
      # selects the ROW ** -- distinct qi means distinct rows; (2) ** within a
      # row the column is n shifted by a PER-ROW CONSTANT **
      # `Starts[row_base + qi]`, so distinct n write distinct columns of that
      # row; (3) ** across pipeline stages `ti` the ABSOLUTE key
      # `key_base + n` is unique**, because tiles partition
      # [start_min, end_max) by `block_n`; (4) ** the grid partitions the rest
      # ** -- `bx` splits rows, `by` splits tiles further across CTAs. And the
      # two bounds guards (`0 <= column < width`) ** DROP writes, never
      # duplicate a coordinate **, so they cannot manufacture a second writer.
      # The checker warns because it ** cannot symbolically correlate the
      # dynamic `Starts[]` load across loop iterations ** and therefore
      # conservatively assumes the worst; the "all zeros" shape it surfaces
      # (rows=1, width=1, n=1, tile_count=1) is its ** degenerate SAT probe,
      # not a serving scenario **.
      # THE FIX, AND WHY IT IS THE RIGHT SHAPE: adds
      # `tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_DATA_RACE_CHECK: True` to ** THIS
      # KERNEL'S jit pass_configs ONLY ** -- the same per-kernel PassContext
      # scope the decorator already uses for TL_DISABLE_TMA_LOWER (and
      # TL_DISABLE_WARP_SPECIALIZED two lines above). NOT a global disable,
      # NOT a check removal for the other kernels in the file: a future REAL
      # race in a sibling kernel still warns.
      # ** API PROOF -- INTEGRATOR-VERIFIED BEYOND THE WORKER'S CLAIM. ** The
      # worker recorded the key's value and consumer; at this seat the whole
      # chain was EXECUTED under CVD=9 with NO kernel compile:
      #   `tilelang.PassConfigKey.TL_DISABLE_DATA_RACE_CHECK` ==
      #     **'tl.disable_data_race_check'**  (transform/pass_config.py:52)
      #   consumer `should_enable_race_check(pass_ctx)`
      #     (backend/pass_pipeline/pipeline_utils.py:46) reads
      #     `not pass_ctx.config.get(TL_DISABLE_DATA_RACE_CHECK, False)`, and
      #     called against real PassContexts it returns ** False WITH our key
      #     ** and ** True WITHOUT it ** -- i.e. the two-state behavior of the
      #     exact knob we set, demonstrated rather than asserted.
      #   `import sglang...qsa.mqa` succeeds with HAS_TILELANG == True, so the
      #     symbol resolves at import (that is all this change needs -- see
      #     next paragraph, nothing is compiled here).
      # ** NO BEHAVIOR CHANGE, AND NO GPU DEBT ADDED: ** the pass key only
      # SKIPS A COMPILE-TIME ANALYSIS PASS; ** codegen is unchanged **, so the
      # generated kernel is byte-identical and there is nothing to re-verify
      # numerically in a GPU window. This is deliberately NOT one of the
      # entries that owes a hardware follow-up.
      # BATTERY -- ** SET-IDENTITY MEASURED BY THE INTEGRATOR AT BOTH TIPS,
      # NOT JUST THE TOTALS: ** qsa dir
      # (test/registered/kernels/ops/attention/qsa/, CVD=9, prod venv,
      # `--continue-on-collection-errors`):
      #   base 065fdcdf33 : 3 failed / 111 passed / 624 skipped / 6 errors
      #   tip  e9f0ae1838 : ** 3 failed / 111 passed / 624 skipped / 6 errors **
      #   -> totals IDENTICAL ** and the 9 FAILED+ERROR NODE IDS ARE
      #   SET-IDENTICAL (diff = EMPTY) **: 3F =
      #   test_qsa.py::test_qsa_trtllm_sparse_decode_arch_gate

      #   test_qsa_file_restart_and_missing_sidecar[pools0],
      #   test_qsa_host_roundtrip_and_consumer_fence[pools0..3]). CAUSE OF ALL
      #   NINE, traced in the log: 12 occurrences of ** `RuntimeError: No CUDA
      #   GPUs are available` ** at torch/cuda/__init__.py:591 -- pre-existing
      #   DEVICE-GATED classes already documented in CAVEATS, untouched by this
      #   pick (as expected for a compile-analysis-only change).
      #   METHOD-DELTA NOTED AND RESOLVED: the worker ran this dir with ** CVD

  * b12x MXFP8 DENSE (entry 316) IS INERT ON BOTH OUR ARMS -- DO NOT READ
    IT AS A DEFAULT CHANGE, AND DO NOT ARM IT WITHOUT THESE TWO CHECKS
    (2026-10-29).
      WHY INERT HERE (the predicate, not a probability):
      can_serve_block_fp8_as_mxfp8 (fp8_utils.py:977-986) requires
      weight_block_size[1] == 32 AND scale_fmt == "ue8m0"; our dense
      projections are BF16 on the NVFP4 arm and block-FP8 [128,128] on the
      FP8 + MTP/NEXTN draft arms, so it is ** SHAPE-FALSE AT EVERY LAYER **
      (live probe: [128,128]/ue8m0 -> False, [128,128]/row -> False,
      [1,128] -> False). Under CVD=9 the resolver clause is additionally
      False for every shape, so the honest claim rests on the SHAPE
      mismatch, which holds on hardware. Neither touched resolver is ever
      CALLED for our models; block-fp8 dispatch (dispatch_w8a8_block_fp8_
      linear) is untouched; the DSv4.1 override keys on an arch that is not
      ours; no new b12x JIT enters our startup; entry 211's Triton cubin
      prewarm does NOT cover b12x (cutlass-DSL kernel, never a Triton cubin)
      -- a real coverage gap for any future arm.
      CHECK 1 -- ** THE GATE-SPLIT TRAP, MEASURED ON THIS BOX: ** the
      requirements gate (fp8_utils.py:844-856) calls get_cuda_version(),
      which parses ** torch.version.cuda ** (= 13.0 here) and so returns
      ** True **; but the default toolkit is ** /usr/local/cuda -> cuda-
      12.9 ** (nvcc 12.9.86; a 13.0 nvcc exists only at /usr/local/cuda-
      13.0). Gate-pass / build-fail is therefore POSSIBLE HERE -- entry-247
      lineage. IF a b12x-dense arm ever boots, this is NEEDLE #1: read the
      cute-DSL JIT's actual toolkit choice, never the gate's verdict, and
      note the first-launch cold compile is NOT prewarmed.
      CHECK 2 -- ROLLBACK IS ARG-LEVEL, NO ENV: --fp8-gemm-backend
      flashinfer_cutlass pins the OLD answer for native-MXFP8 dense, ANY
      non-auto pin ALSO vetoes the DSv4.1 override, and the K%128 guard
      degrades ineligible layers to Triton PER LAYER with a named warning
      (never a crash). Where it DOES flip: `auto` AND any pin that does not
      own an MXFP8 dense kernel (plain "cutlass"/"triton" fall through to
      auto by the function's own contract) -> flashinfer_b12x; explicit
      flashinfer_cutlass is unchanged on both trees; flashinfer_cutedsl
      still raises on SM120. For a NATIVE-MXFP8 checkpoint that is auto->
      b12x LIVE AT DEFAULT -- but the base's flashinfer_cutlass there was a
      DEAD PATH (cutlass/cute-dsl MXFP8 hard-fail on SM120/121, tcgen05/
      TMEM absent), so this converts broken into working rather than
      disturbing a working default. STILL-DECLINED CONTEXT UNREOPENED:
      entry 225's refusal of #38170's FP4 auto->b12x flip and entry 240's
      keep-auto-cutlass posture are mm_fp4 territory, untouched by this
      mm_mxfp8 family.
      UNTESTED BY CONSTRUCTION: the PR ships NO tests, so nothing on this
      route is pinned until ** S-B12XDENSE ** (wanted_stack, filed this
      pass) lands -- owed WHEN AND IF the route is armed, entry-247's
      mock-wheel idiom.

  * HICACHE STORAGE LIVENESS (entry 318) -- TWO THINGS TO KNOW BEFORE
    READING A DEGRADED BOOT, PLUS THE NEW GAUGE (2026-10-29).
      THE GAUGE: ** sglang:hicache_storage_healthy = 1 STEADY **
      (metrics_collector.py:2303-2304; .set(int(healthy)) at :2397) -> the
      §3(l)-adjacent boot needle. ** 0 means the RANK-AGREED health bit
      dropped and L3 has DEGRADED TO NO-L3 under the default "disable"
      policy: requests KEEP SUCCEEDING, WITHOUT L3, AND PROGRESS ON
      IN-FLIGHT PREFETCHES IS FROZEN. ** That is precisely the failure mode
      that used to be SILENT, so: a healthy-looking server with a flat
      L3-hit rate is now a GAUGE question first, not a traffic question.
      The gauge also DROPS ON DETACH (commit B), so a deliberate detach is
      NOT an alarm.
      THE ALWAYS-ENTER INVARIANT (the invariant that makes the fix correct
      rather than cosmetic): ** NEVER skip a storage sync collective after
      a failure. ** gloo closes only the PAIR OF THE GROUP THAT TIMED OUT,
      and the hit and completion groups are DISTINCT EVEN ON TP2, so a
      skip-then-proceed design leaves a non-failed peer blocked for the
      UNTOUCHED group's FULL timeout (30 min at torch defaults) during which
      reset/detach cannot join and flush RAISES OUT OF THE SCHEDULER; a retry
      on the broken group raises AT ONCE on every rank, which is why the
      collective is always entered. Anyone "optimising" the failure path by
      skipping work re-introduces the 30-minute wedge.
      RELATED INVARIANTS ON THE RECORD: ** FLAG-THEN-PUBLISH ** for the
      health bit (folded AFTER the queue-size reads; a counted failed item
      implies the 0 bit IN THE SAME ROUND -- publish-then-flag would let a
      rank vote on an item whose failure it had not recorded); storage cut
      rides the ** deadline machinery with NO poll_miss re-arm ** so entry
      293's shared retry-max-attempts cap stays untouched and the timeout
      stop policy stays the EARLIER exit; ** storage_sync_timeout now
      DEFAULTS TO 300 s ** (absent/null -> default, non-finite rejected) so
      a hung peer is noticed in MINUTES -- and the PP ticket group is created
      at worker-start so it is BOUNDED LIKE THE OTHERS (it used to be built
      before the config parsed); ** shm row width is 9 for the hicache
      exchange ** (width 8 on intermediate commits meant storage rounds took
      the gloo fallback -- a tree stuck at 8 is a PARTIAL port); and
      ** extra-config validation is NON-CONSUMING: registration validates,
      _apply_storage_sync_config APPLIES -- the sole application point. **
      GPU DEBT: the kill -STOP file-backend-reader drill on TP4 (peers time
      out within 300 s, threads SURVIVE, gauge drops ONE tick, re-attach
      recovers) -- unreachable on this box.

        exceed maximum number of retries" -- ** ONE CAUSE, TWO TEXTS **: the

        ** THE LOADER NEEDS NO SHELL ENV AT ALL -- IMPORTANT, AND THE GUARD
        AGAINST MISREADING THE RECIPE ABOVE. **
        rust_extensions/torch_build.py:27-101 derives the entire environment
        from the RUNNING INTERPRETER: it POPS LIBTORCH_USE_PYTORCH, points

        LIBTORCH_BYPASS_VERSION_CHECK=1, the CXX11 ABI flag, LD_LIBRARY_PATH,

        path), plus RUSTFLAGS rpaths -- the artifact fingerprint even hashes the

        THE ROOT CAUSE: the historical "cargo build ... exit 101" occurred WITH a

    #45543's own helper) but deliberately ** does NOT change the cache key**,
    so extend-bucket records tuned on all-zero dummies remain VALID and KEEP
    HITTING under the existing v2_default key. THE SEQUENCING RULE, in boot
    terms: entry 260's cache-key bump means the NEXT restart is already
    scheduled-cold, and it absorbs the S-EXT1 retune for free -- that is why
    this pick was landed BEFORE the restart, and why the boot you want is
    the cold one. ** IF A WARM NEW-KEY BOOT ALREADY HAPPENED, the extend

  * ENVREG (entry 309) IS AN ACCEPTANCE-SEMANTICS CHANGE, NOT A NO-OP
    REFACTOR (2026-10-29, operator-approved). Three comm envs moved from raw
    os.environ.get to environ.py registration, and EnvBool/EnvInt PARSE
    DIFFERENTLY from the raw sites: ** `true`, `yes`, `y` (any case) NOW ARM
    where only the exact string "1" did, and a GARBAGE value now
    warns-and-falls-back-to-default where MAX_BYTES used to raise int() and
    TOOK THE COORDINATOR __init__ DOWN WITH IT. ** Verified LIVE on the
    landed tree (see the re-pin table). Two consequences to hold in mind:
    (i) an operator who wrote =true and got nothing before now gets the
    transport they clearly meant -- that is the intent, and it is a
    BEHAVIOR CHANGE on the record, not byte-identical; (ii) the discovered
    NON-UNIFORMITY explains why raw reads were load-bearing at all: the LL
    gate checked IN-BAND PER CALL while the host-staged gate checked at
    MODULE-IMPORT TIME, so sibling switches had different liveness rules;
    registration unifies the parse while ** envs.get still reads live
    os.environ, so setenv-driven boots behave the same and the manual GPU
    tests are unaffected **. SGLANG_HOST_STAGED_ALLREDUCE_MIN_BYTES and
    ..._PIECE were LEFT RAW ON PURPOSE (the latter is an import-time read;
    registering it needs its own callable-default decision). MERGE NOTE:
    entry 256's #39605 is an OPEN port -- if a merge ever pulls upstream's
    raw version of these sites, THIS registration must be re-applied.

  * #43216 BOOT-NEEDLE BULLET -- THE NCCL ENVIRONMENT OF EVERY FUTURE
    STACK BOOT IS DIFFERENT FROM ALL PRIOR BOOTs (entry 284, landed
    2026-10-28; arg_groups/parallel_hook.py). NCCL_GRAPH_REGISTER=0 is now
    setdefault'd on EVERY multi-rank graph-capable boot -- THE 9.sh TP4
    ARM INCLUDED, not just TP2 -- because NCCL recycles graph-pool
    temporaries under expandable_segments and the older guards missed our
    shape. WHAT ACTUALLY MOVED (verified in code, 2026-10-29, because the
    handoff note got this wrong -- ** CORRECTION ON RECORD: the claim
    "no explicit-override warning / an operator setting silently wins" IS
    FALSE. The warning EXISTS and PREDATES THIS PICK: the
    `if os.environ.setdefault("NCCL_GRAPH_REGISTER","0") != "0":
    logger.warning("NCCL_GRAPH_REGISTER=%s was set explicitly; %s.")`
    block at parallel_hook.py:445-449 was ALREADY there at the pre-284
    tip (it came in with the #38936-era LM-head guard); #43216 did not
    touch it. An explicit operator setting DOES win AND DOES WARN **).
    The pick's real content is the TRIGGER: two narrow guards
    (enable_tp_lm_head_all_to_all; enable_memory_saver AND
    SGLANG_MEMORY_SAVER_CUDA_GRAPH) were REPLACED by
    _graph_may_capture_collectives() = max(tp,ep,dp) > 1 AND
    (cuda_graph_config is None OR ANY phase backend != DISABLED),
    documented to "err toward True" -- so ** our entry-#38936-era
    _graph_pool_is_pausable helper was DELETED as a strict subset of that
    predicate (no dangling callers, grep-verified); if a future fork
    change ever needs the narrow pausable-pool trigger again, it now has
    to re-derive it from the general predicate.** THREE OPERATIONAL
    CONSEQUENCES: (i) read the warning line above as a LIVE NEEDLE -- if a
    boot prints it, someone exported NCCL_GRAPH_REGISTER in the run script
    or shell and THEIR value wins by design (legitimate, logged, not a
    hook failure); (ii) ** any old needle asserting the ABSENCE of
    NCCL_GRAPH_REGISTER in a boot env dump is now STALE -- keep a pre-boot
    dump and compare (entry-245's needle-string trap applies: assert on
    strings that actually print);** (iii) THE FIRST BOOT AFTER THIS LANDS
    OWES A DECODE-GRAPH HANG CHECK: this fix's own symptom class is graph
    replay of captured cross-rank collectives spinning in
    ncclDevKernel_SendRecv while DP ranks ramp -- reproduced upstream on
    tp4/dp4/ep4, i.e. OUR shape -- so if a new hang/stall appears inside a
    captured graph, suspect #43216 first and test by exporting
    NCCL_GRAPH_REGISTER=1 (which now also prints the warning, giving a
    clean A/B). Merge-#5 note: this is a MERGED pick, patch-equal to
    upstream 786cdfc040, so the merge brings nothing new here.

    THE GENERALIZATION WORTH KEEPING: a memo key that includes a CALLER-CONTEXT
      argument (a length, a device, a mode) is a silent per-caller cache
      multiplication, and the duplicated objects are usually bit-identical -- so
      nothing ever looks wrong, you just pay twice. When two model copies (target
      + draft) are built from one process-global builder, check what the KEY
      contains versus what the CONSTRUCTOR receives. And fix the KEY, not the
      upstream value: the rewrite that made them differ was load-bearing.

    assert_pkg_version("0.7.2") in srt/entrypoints/engine.py); taking
    that code while the prod venv stays on 0.7.1 HARD-FAILS boot, and
    the venv bump is the operator's call, so our pin + engine assert
    stay at 0.7.1 (requirements/pyproject + engine.py:1925 verified).
      [10-19 LATER THE SAME DAY: REVERSED by the operator's decision --
      2b46270c5d takes 44be30d216/#44591 and the prod venv moved to
      0.7.2. Full record in entry 228. Keep the reasoning as history but
      note that it was direction-correct and mechanism-wrong: direction-
      correct because the pin and the wheel must never be left split,
      mechanism-wrong because assert_pkg_version is a FLOOR, not an
      equality check (utils/common.py:2380, the parameter is literally
      named min_version) -- so new wheel + old pin BOOTS, and the box in
      fact ran that way for hours, while the pin going UP past an old
      wheel is what hard-fails boot. The reason the pin nevertheless had
      to land is quieter: the operator's rebuild command
      `uv pip install -e 'python[dev]'` re-resolves the `==0.7.1` pin and
      would have SILENTLY DOWNGRADED flashinfer back to 0.7.1 (run from
      /root/sglang/sglang, uv walks UP to the prod /root/sglang/.venv --
      there is no .venv in the repo root). Deferring the code pin until
      the wheel actually moved was sound; the stated failure mode was
      not the real one.]

  * BEHAVIOR WATCH -- NEXT RESTART (entries 248-249): FOUR THINGS, ONE OF WHICH
    IS A COUPLING THAT DID NOT EXIST BEFORE.
      - TARGET AND DRAFT NOW SHARE ONE ROPE CACHE (entry 248). They got the same
        ANSWERS before (the tensors were bit-identical), but they now share the
        SAME OBJECT, so anything that mutates it mutates both: the in-place bf16
        re-cast in _match_cos_sin_cache_dtype and any growth in
        _ensure_cos_sin_cache_length. Benign on today's config (both models are
        bf16; a cache too SHORT for either still gets its own entry because rows
        are in the key). The watch is for the day a per-model dtype, a different
        rotary_dim, or a per-model cache length is introduced -- that change is
        now a change to a shared object, and it will not announce itself as a
        rope change. Symptom to look for: one model's rope table behaving like the
        other's. Rollback is one commit (1818e2c0fb), revert-alone.
      - THE SEED PIN DOES NOTHING FOR GREEDY TRAFFIC (entry 249). If a harness
        re-run produces byte-identical numbers with and without --random-seed,
        that is EXPECTED: greedy bypasses the RNG and the harness benches at
        temperature 0.0. Judge the pin on temp>0/top_p<1 traffic and on the BOOT
        PATH (draft init_new, DeepGEMM warmup, autotuner RNG), whose whole purpose
        is that two boots become comparable. And never accept a pinned-seed green
        KL as an accuracy verdict by itself.
      - THE L3 STORE WILL GROW, ON PURPOSE, TO ~510 GB AGGREGATE (entry 249). Cap
        is now 150G PER RANK with a 127.5 GB/rank watermark, up from ~3.0-3.9 h of
        warm retention to ~9.1-11.8 h. Judge it with `df -B1 /root/hicache_store`
        and `/proc/<pid>/environ`. What the log does and does not tell you: each
        rank prints ONE INFO line at boot, "HiCacheFile eviction enabled:
        cap=150000000000 B, watermark=..., min_free=..., existing=... B (N
        entries)" (lru_file_evictor.py:121-125) -- grep THAT to confirm the cap
        landed. METADATA_TTL is never logged at all, and per-file unlink
        decisions are logger.debug, so "I see no eviction lines" proves nothing;
        absence of INFO eviction chatter is the normal state. The 100 GB GLOBAL
        MIN_FREE_SPACE is the real ceiling and the only knob that can make
        writes start refusing; watch it as the aggregate approaches ~1.7 TB used.
        A cap RAISE is free at boot; a LOWERING below a rank's holding is the
        blocking-unlink storm -- do not lower it casually.
      - POWER HEADROOM IS ON HALF THE BOX ONLY (entry 249 §D, corrected).
        max_limit is 325 W on GPU2/GPU3 and 300 W on GPU0/GPU1, and all four sit
        at 300 W today -- so the two cards that are throttling hardest CANNOT be
        raised. The box is genuinely SW-power-capped at 70-74% of clocks at 98%
        util with no thermal component, but a TP4 step is lockstep and bounded by
        the slowest rank, so `-pl 325 -i 2,3` is a LOW-EV operator experiment,
        not a lever. Both run scripts' existing comments on this are correct.
      - STANDING RESTART CAVEAT. The running FP4 boot (pid 4058818, 10-21
        17:42:20) already carries HiCache ON, --hicache-mamba-size-gb auto,
        --mem-fraction-static 0.88 and --max-mamba-cache-size 384 -- so the
        "HiCache/L3 is PARKED" statements in entries 245/246 are HISTORY, and
        max_total_num_tokens=8,012,096 CONFIRMS the sizing model. What is still
        restart-pending after this batch, and nothing else: SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_-
        BACKEND_MAX_SIZE (live 50G), SGLANG_HICACHE_FILE_BACKEND_METADATA_TTL
        (live 300), --random-seed (absent from the live argv), and entry 248's
        rope dedup. All four land on the next restart the USER drives; until then
        a boot-level claim about them is unverifiable, and re-reading the current
        log cannot show any of them.
  * BEHAVIOR WATCH -- NEXT RESTART (entry 247): A NEW MoE BACKEND IS SELECTABLE
    AND IT IS NOT IN USE. `--moe-runner-backend flashinfer_b12x_w4a16` exists in
    the CLI choices, `auto` still resolves to flashinfer_cutlass on SM120 (pinned
    by test_auto_never_resolves_to_b12x), and NEITHER run script names the new
    backend. Nothing to observe this restart unless someone opts in -- but if a
    log DOES show any "b12x W4A16" line on a boot that did not pass the flag, that
    is a resolution bug, not a feature working. Two things to remember before
    arming it: it needs CUDA_HOME=/usr/local/cuda-13.0 (a per-process repin whose
    first boot pays a ~4-5 minute flashinfer re-JIT, see the entry), and it is
    UNMEASURED -- expected bs1 gain is roughly zero because EP4 always passes an
    expert_map, which disqualifies flashinfer's two fastest small-M paths. Its
    reason to exist is acceptance-length recovery and bs>=8, both still owed.
  * BEHAVIOR WATCH -- NEXT RESTART (entries 244-246): BOTH ARMS BOOT WITH
    PCIe-IPC ALL-REDUCE FOR THE FIRST TIME. Neither arm has ever run this
    transport (prod logs contain zero PCIe-IPC lines to date), so the first
    thing to do at that boot is READ THE NEEDLES, not benchmark.
      - Expected per boot: 4x "FlashInfer PCIe-IPC all-reduce enabled",
        4x "FlashInfer PCIe-IPC workspace built (world=4, max_numel=491520,
        hidden=2560)", 4x "...all-reduce autotuned N shape(s)", and UP TO 8x
        "PCIe-IPC vocab gather: up to 384 rows x 62080". THE VOCAB GATHER IS
        TWO PER RANK, because target and draft each build their own gatherer --
        so 4 lines is NOT partial success, it means the NEXTN DRAFT is still on
        NCCL inside the graph (an unmeasured gap; record which you got).
        0 enabled lines = the gate never opened. 8 enabled lines = the
        group-aliasing model (one `tp` coordinator for attn-TP/MoE-EP/TP) is
        wrong; re-derive before trusting anything else.
      - THE HARD PASS/FAIL IS `autotuned N shape(s)` WITH N > 0 (expected 9).
        An UNTUNED seed policy can be SLOWER than NCCL, so a boot that merely
        came up is not a pass. Four distinct declines exist and none of them is
        the ordinary path: "autotune covered no shapes", "autotune failed
        (...); keeping the seed policy", "no host group to autotune on",
        "--disable-flashinfer-autotune is set" -- precondition: the dump shows
        'disable_flashinfer_autotune': False.
      - FIRST BOOT COST, both arms: a one-time flashinfer JIT for the PCIe-IPC
        kernels plus a nine-shape autotune pass, cached per flashinfer version +
        arch. Judge startup and tok/s from the SECOND boot and write down which
        boot a number came from.
      - Needle traps (a wrong grep here reads as a clean boot): the setup
        failure says "allreduce" WITHOUT a hyphen; "fixed fallback width 64" is
        invisible to a SOURCE grep but prints; "reductions stay on NCCL" is both
        a benign per-dtype note AND a real transport disable; "keeping the seed
        policy" is shared by three declines; "multimem all-gather disabled (no
        multicast for world_size=4)" fires whether or not the change works.
    THE FP4 ARM ADDITIONALLY CHANGES OUTPUT ORDER. DELTA 4
    (SGLANG_FLASHINFER_MOE_FUSED_FINALIZE=1) fuses the top-k reduce into the
    GEMM2 epilogue and accumulates ATOMICALLY: not bit-reproducible run-to-run

    SILENTLY FORCED BACK TO 0 by --enable-deterministic-inference. So a small
    accept-length or logprob move on that arm is expected until the (r) accuracy
    gate runs, and a "determinism made my win vanish" mystery starts here.
    DELTA 3 removes four log lines people have been told to look for: with

    LIMITATION, on purpose: a prefill OOM stays FATAL where paged cannot

    SGLANG_ENABLE_QSA_CHUNK_PREFILL_OOM_FALLBACK=0 restore pre-fix
    behaviour; the real prevention (paged at boot) is a script decision.

    the same registry.
